## Earlier handoff note — 2026-11-24 Feature 001 A-124 recovery implementation

- **Checkout:** started from `main` at `a2f366cec8165c58f6fc57930733fdc4c842184f`, equal to `origin/main`, with a clean worktree and one worktree. This A-124 slice is currently uncommitted; no commit or push has been made.
- **Scope and decision:** implements the user-delegated A-124 direction under Feature 001 T03/T04, without changing the external owner status, public API, event contract, migration or schema. The pinned OpenCode v2.0.32 protocol sources are recorded in Feature 001 RESEARCH §10.
- **Code path:** prompt acknowledgement is accepted only when returned `data.id`, `data.sessionID` and `data.type` exactly match the submitted message, session and `user` type. On `StopUnconfirmed` only, the adapter reads the same process/session's durable log at `after=0&follow=true`, capped at five seconds, then passes a correlated terminal observation through existing Conversation settlement. The bounded reducer verifies replay watermark, event envelope and order, prompt enqueue/delivery, execution start, complete assistant text parts, and one terminal outcome. It never resends, reads deltas/reasoning/tool payloads/raw errors, or turns ambiguous evidence into success. Unresolved evidence preserves the existing cancellation state and retained process/session ownership.
- **CODE evidence so far:** parser cases passed 11/11, including exact recovered-text preservation; the pinned recovery route test passed 1/1; subscription-before-prompt ordering passed 1/1. After increasing only fake-server fixture waits (production recovery remains capped at five seconds), `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked agents::opencode_ -- --nocapture` passed **35 passed / 0 failed / 1 intentionally ignored** on native Windows 11 Home Single Language build 26300. The foreign prompt-receipt rejection is included in that run and also passed in its focused test.
- **Evidence boundary:** these are parser and fake-transport CODE tests. There is no authenticated OpenCode turn, recovered-outcome SQLite integration test, proof that the retained host handle is retired/recovered after settlement, packaged Send/Stop/retry/restart journey, or USER qualification. T03/T04 remain PARTIAL; T09/T23 remain open.
- **Documentation and contract checks:** after the structural validator found that the first new WIRING edges reused existing IDs `e38`/`e39`, the new edges were assigned unused IDs `e42`/`e43`; the final `.specify/scripts/validate_feature_package.py` run passed **20/20**. `scripts/validate_design_owner_consistency.py` read **380 documents / 82,869 lines**, ran 36 assertions and found **0 issues**; `scripts/audit_v1_design_depth.py --strict` reported **0 findings** across 375 files / 81,308 lines; `scripts/audit_v1_markdown.py` checked 369 files / 81,136 lines / 8,890,379 bytes with **0 broken links**; strict API audit reported **0 errors** across 236 operations (184 served, 4 outside OpenAPI, 52 target-only); served-route inventory matched **184/4/52**; WIRING YAML parsed; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed; and `git -c core.whitespace=cr-at-eol diff --check` passed with only Git's line-ending normalization notices. ## 2026-11-23 latest continuation — Feature 001-T32 Runtime readiness fence implemented

- **Delivery:** committed as `ee5a0098a7c09434c9534660da5e506e98767005` (`fix(conversation): gate admission on runtime readiness`) and pushed normally to `origin/main`. Post-push verification confirmed local `HEAD` and `refs/heads/main` both equal `ee5a0098a7c09434c9534660da5e506e98767005`; the worktree was clean. Before the slice, branch `main` was at `3913d2298fbcdeae871e449891d494b18b0eb229` with a clean tree and one registered worktree.
- **Decision and scope:** A-119 is USER-DELEGATED implementation direction, not Conversation/Runtime/Storage owner ratification. Feature 001 revision 1.2 remains IN_REVIEW. T32 implements only the bounded operational readiness fence; it does not authenticate or qualify a provider and does not close T09/T28.
- **Implementation:** the authenticated daemon Operator supplies the process-owned exact `{runtime_id, runtime_incarnation_id}` internally for New Chat first-send, existing-Conversation Send and Retry. Storage resolves exact committed replay first, completes read-only identity, ownership, version, Resource and binding checks, then checks in the same SQLite IMMEDIATE transaction that the exact Runtime is current and ONLINE and its incarnation is READY with `ready_at` set and `stopped_at` absent. This fence sits immediately before the first durable/blob effect. Missing, stale, superseded, offline, degraded, unknown or malformed readiness returns existing `503 RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE` without new message, turn, event, intent, snapshot or request receipt. Exact committed replay remains recoverable after degradation. ## 2026-11-19 continuation — Feature 001 T29 atomic New Chat first-send

- **Decision and scope:** T29 records the product direction delegated by the user: New Chat's first Send must atomically admit the exact first message, not create a title-only Conversation and keep the message in ephemeral process memory for a second click. This is USER-DELEGATED DIRECTION, not Conversation/Storage/API domain-owner ratification, package acceptance, external protocol evidence or release qualification.
- **Cross-layer path implemented:** the New Chat Send control builds a stable key from the exact body, selected Workspace and displayed AgentBinding (including the visible Workspace default), retains ResourceRefs and presentation preference, and invokes one typed Tauri command. The Tauri bridge persists the bounded exact request in the private v2 create journal (v1 entries remain readable), then sends authenticated `POST /v1/conversations/first-turn`. The Operator verifies Workspace ownership, eligibility and ResourceRefs; SQLite rechecks owner, Workspace and eligible binding inside `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; one transaction writes the Conversation and rev-1 snapshot/event, first USER message, OPEN turn and turn event, rev-2 Conversation snapshot/message event, dispatch intent, and exact request receipt. Dispatch remains outside the SQLite transaction through the existing coordinator. The response includes a composite Conversation/turn receipt; the UI and bridge clear only after validating it. A definitive typed precommit 4xx clears that journal entry while preserving the draft for correction under a new key; ambiguity and 5xx retain the exact request.
- **Storage contract correction from red test:** the first focused daemon run returned `INVALID_ARGUMENT` because first-send reused the normal append-turn validator, whose request digest shape is intentionally different. The shared immutable message/turn/event record checks are now separate from per-operation request-receipt validation, and the storage owner verifies the exact first-send payload against the committed title, content, ResourceRefs, selected binding and preference. The earlier regression (before the added denial case) then passed atomic admission, exact replay and changed-payload conflict; its counts were one Conversation, one message, one turn, one receipt, one intent and three canonical events.
- **Focused evidence:** `node --experimental-transform-types --test tests/conversation-attachments.test.ts tests/conversation-view.test.ts` in `apps/litecowork-ui` passed **31/31**. On native Windows, `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked new_chat_first_send_commits_and_replays_conversation_and_turn_atomically -- --nocapture` passed **1/1**, including foreign-owner and mismatched-Workspace denial, ineligible-binding and missing-Resource denial with zero partial rows, correction under the unused key, one atomic admission, exact persisted composite-receipt equality after SQLite reopen, duplicate suppression and changed-payload conflict. Tauri `conversation_create_journal` passed **9/9** and `create_recovery_tests` passed **3/3**, covering v1 compatibility, exact pending-request replay, bounded/private storage behavior, corruption refusal, precise acknowledgment, definitive typed 4xx clearing and retention after transport/5xx/malformed success. These tests establish CODE and the mounted authenticated route is SERVED; they do not establish packaged desktop/provider integration. Strict route audit reports 184 OpenAPI-served / 4 outside OpenAPI / 52 target-only across 236 operations; strict contract and design-depth audits report zero errors/findings. The refreshed Markdown audit reports 369 included files / 79,477 lines / zero broken links; the active corpus reports 372 files / 79,451 lines.

## 2026-11-19 continuation — Feature 001 T24 pinned OpenCode event contract

- **Decision and scope:** the user delegated implementation of owner-decision gaps. For T24, the decision is to implement the exact pinned OpenCode v2.0.32 source contract and keep real provider behavior claims behind T23 and T09. This is USER-DELEGATED DIRECTION, not owner ratification, provider evidence or release qualification.
- **Source-backed behavior:** the normalizer reads the event discriminator from the JSON envelope `type`, session correlation only from `data.sessionID`, drops unknown/foreign/uncorrelated frames, and excludes reasoning content. Step start/end/failure remain distinct. The adapter holds the same `/api/event` SSE stream that was opened successfully before dispatching the Conversation prompt; if opening it fails, no prompt is sent. OpenCode step token counts are per-step observations, while `session.usage.updated` reports cumulative native-session totals, so the per-turn accumulator includes only terminal step counts. No file/todo activity is invented from unsupported event names or `session.step.ended.files[]` metadata.

## 2026-11-19 continuation — Held Environment launch and preparation-journal CODE

- Added a separate held-runner mode to `litecowork-scope-gate`: it reports readiness inside the exact Bubblewrap namespace, waits before creating the native harness, and accepts only one exact post-admission signal. `HeldEnvironmentScope` exposes native stdio only after a successful signal; a failed signal write is ambiguous and cannot be retried. Existing candidate-capture mode remains immediate and `linux_environment_provider` explicitly keeps that mode for its existing capture path.
- Added `RuntimeLocalPreparationJournal` under stable Runtime-private storage. It writes intent before external preparation, records the exact held-scope identity and admission receipt, persists one-shot handoff attempts, and classifies receipt lookup as unknown/block, definitely absent/inspect exact scope, or exact commit/recover the committed Attempt. The journal is not a domain record, API, event, backup object or authorization source; it does not itself query SQLite or a provider.
- Verification: native-Windows `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked environment_isolation::tests -- --nocapture` passed **22/22** using the toolchain `rust-lld` linker; the target emitted 29 compiler warnings, mostly existing unused/dead-code warnings in unrelated modules. The opt-in direct WSL `held_runner_does_not_start_native_harness_until_admission_signal` integration passed **1/1**. These are CODE-level unit/direct-provider tests only.
- Pre-integration code-review gates: `list_pending` currently reads `.json` paths without the same no-follow regular-file, owner/mode and canonical attempt-key filename checks as `load`; journal phase read-modify-write operations have no shared or cross-process serialization. ## 2026-11-18 continuation — Feature 005 T04 Bubblewrap scratch mount correction

- The earlier direct fixed-command tmpfs probe was replaced with a live check of `prepare_bubblewrap_command`. That exposed a production launch-plan defect: Bubblewrap resolves `--bind` sources in the host namespace, but the builder tried to bind `/workspace/tmp`, which exists only inside the sandbox. A minimal WSL reproduction confirmed the launch failed before the agent command ran.
- The builder now creates `/tmp` as an in-sandbox symlink to `/workspace/tmp`, keeping output, home and temporary writes on the same capped `/workspace` tmpfs without a host bind. The opt-in regression reads back the exact 1 MiB filesystem cap, writes 256 KiB under `/workspace/outputs`, writes through `/tmp`, then observes `ENOSPC` while both paths consume the same quota.
- WSL Ubuntu 24.04: `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=1 cargo test -p linux-process-scope --locked --test bubblewrap_scope bubblewrap_command_builder_enforces_workspace_tmpfs_cap_and_observes_enospc -- --ignored --nocapture` passed **1/1**; `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=1 cargo test -p linux-process-scope --locked` passed **36 library + 4 runner tests**, with 3 live library checks and all 3 Bubblewrap/systemd integration checks ignored by default. `cargo fmt -p linux-process-scope -- --check` passed on Windows.
- Documentation checks after the correction: Feature 005 package validation passed for 20 packages; architecture validation passed with the existing warning that optional `openapi-spec-validator` is not installed; Markdown audit found 373 files / 80,367 lines / 0 broken links. The live route audit matched 183 served / 4 outside OpenAPI / 52 target-only; strict V1 contract, owner-consistency and design-depth audits each report only their stale expected route-count tuple (181/4/53 or 179/4/54). CRLF-aware `git diff --check` passed.
- Evidence is CODE for the production Bubblewrap argument builder run directly under WSL. It still bypasses `ManagedScope`, systemd/cgroup and daemon EnvironmentConfig mapping; it does not attest or admit an Environment or persist a proposal. Native Linux, packaged Task execution and QUALIFIED evidence remain open. T04 remains PARTIAL.

## 2026-11-18 continuation — Feature 005 T04 direct Bubblewrap runner integration

- Added an opt-in integration test in `crates/linux-process-scope/tests/bubblewrap_scope.rs` and ran it explicitly on WSL Ubuntu 24.04 with `cargo test -p linux-process-scope --locked --test bubblewrap_scope candidate_runner_as_pid_one_blocks_frame_forgery_and_waits_for_descendants -- --ignored --nocapture`; **1/1 passed**. It launches the built scope-gate binary directly inside Bubblewrap with the runner as PID 1, has the child attempt to write a forged `LCOW` frame through `/proc/1/fd/2`, and has a reparented descendant write the real proposal after the parent exits. - This is WSL-only CODE evidence. It bypasses `ManagedScope` and systemd/cgroup supervision, uses a locally built runner rather than an installed root-owned package binary, and produces no Environment attestation, Attempt admission, Resource, or Task receipt. Native packaged Linux qualification remains open.

## 2026-11-18 continuation — Feature 005 T04 candidate capture retry denial

- Added a private daemon capture session after tracing the candidate-export contract and the actual `ManagedScope`/scope-gate pipes. It starts the mapped Bubblewrap launch, retains the native harness stdin/stdout streams for its existing ACP/protocol client, and drains the distinct bounded stderr candidate channel on a reader thread while the exchange runs. Malformed channel data is not retained; the reader drains the remaining pipe to avoid blocking the trusted runner.
- A source re-audit found that a nonzero child exit failed the first capture attempt but left the parsed frame available to a second call. Added a terminal exit-status latch and joined/discarded the candidate reader on nonzero exit. A failed-exit session can no longer be retried into a candidate; quiescence timeout remains retryable on the same scope. Failed native exchange and cancellation continue to discard candidate bytes.
- The regression was first run against the old source and failed by returning `Candidate(CapturedCandidate { bytes: "nonzero exit", ... })` on retry; after the fix, the focused regression passed. WSL Ubuntu 24.04: `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=1 cargo test -p litecoworkd --locked linux_environment_provider` passed **8/8** (723 filtered out), including the retry-after-nonzero-exit regression. Rustfmt check passed for the Linux provider source. Existing unrelated daemon warnings remain.
- Live-provider limitation confirmed in this WSL instance: Bubblewrap 0.9.0 and systemd 255 are installed, but `systemctl is-system-running` reports `degraded`, `/proc/self/cgroup` is `0::/non-systemd`, and `/sys/fs/cgroup` is not writable. The systemd-owned cgroup/quiescence path cannot be exercised here; this is an environment limitation, not Linux qualification.
- Candidate bytes are returned only after `ManagedScope` reports recursive cgroup-v2 quiescence and reaps the direct launcher. Cancellation closes native streams, invokes bounded kill-and-wait, joins/discards the candidate reader and preserves the scope handle when cleanup is uncertain. The returned value binds bytes to Environment/Workspace/Task/Runtime incarnation, root digests and Step; it is transient data only and creates no Resource, attestation, grant, Effect or admission.
- Updated Feature 005 TASKS/SPEC/RESEARCH/error recovery, `docs/ENVIRONMENTS.md`, implementation status/master plan and this handoff. **Evidence:** CODE only. The helper has no production Task Runtime caller; the systemd-managed Bubblewrap scope, cgroup quiescence through `ManagedScope`, installed root-owned runner identity, attestation, Attempt admission, Resource persistence, restart recovery and packaged Linux Task flow remain untested/unintegrated. The direct WSL runner test is not native Linux qualification. No staging, commit or push; unrelated dirty files and `audit-output/` remain preserved.

## 2026-11-18 continuation — Feature 005 T06 B-18 file replacement primitive

- Re-read the B-18 owner contract and traced the primitive's boundary. `replace_approved_file` performs only confined filesystem replacement; it creates no grant, ApprovalUse, Invocation/Effect state, receipt, Environment attestation or Task success. It has no production caller until the separate T08 mediated-dispatch path exists.
- Linux resolves paths from held directory descriptors with `openat2` no-follow/no-cross-device constraints, copies UID/GID, mode, POSIX access ACL and `user.*` xattrs, rejects unsupported xattr namespaces, flushes staged bytes, uses same-directory `renameat`, then fsyncs the parent. Windows uses a narrowly scoped replacement handle, handle-relative no-reparse operations, NTFS-only `FILE_WRITE_THROUGH`, and `NtSetInformationFile(FileRenameInformationEx)`; it copies owner/group/DACL, DACL protection state and the supported DOS attributes. Both perform a final base-digest check and return ambiguity for uncertain replacement outcomes. That check is not a filesystem CAS: external native writers can race the rename outside LiteCowork's lease fence.
- Verification on Windows 11 Home Single Language build 26300 x64: `cargo test -j 1 -p local-environment-staging --locked file_replace -- --nocapture` passed **9/9**; `cargo test -j 1 -p windows-local-security --locked handle_relative_atomic_replacement_preserves_security_and_exact_content -- --nocapture` passed **1/1**; `cargo check -j 1 --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --locked -p windows-local-security -p local-environment-staging` passed. The replacement test covers exact bytes/digest, root-relative confinement, hardlink/reparse refusal and child-process kill after temp flush; the helper test verifies owner/group/DACL and HIDDEN/ARCHIVE preservation.
- Verification on WSL Ubuntu 24.04: `cargo test -j 1 -p local-environment-staging --locked file_replace -- --nocapture` passed **11/11**, including mode/UID/GID and user-xattr preservation plus unsupported-xattr classification. The POSIX ACL-copy branch is not yet verified. A forced `LITECOWORK_REQUIRE_ACL_XATTR_TEST=1` probe failed at ACL setup because the WSL ext4 `/tmp`, tmpfs and a disposable ext4 loop mount returned `ENOTSUP`; the mount/image were cleaned up by the probe trap. Do not count the normal conditional test as ACL evidence.
- Feature 005 T06 remains **IN PROGRESS / CODE only** until the ACL path has a capable Linux test host and its owner acceptance is reviewed. There is still no Task dispatcher, Environment provider, reconciler, independent verifier, packaged Task journey or `INTEGRATED`/`QUALIFIED` evidence. Windows Task execution remains unavailable under B-35. Updated the Feature 005 owner package, Task Runtime, Environments, Storage, implementation status and this handoff to capture the implementation and evidence limits.

## 2026-11-17 continuation — Feature 001 T14 paginated Resource picker

The active T14 task is PARTIAL and explicitly allows the Conversation page, attachment helper and adjacent UI tests. Source tracing confirmed that Tauri `list_resources` already returns `nextCursor` from the authenticated local `GET /v1/resources` page route (100 items per page), but the composer requested only `cursor: null` and discarded the cursor. This was a picker defect; no new route, schema, permission or Workspace-root behavior was needed.

The composer now follows `nextCursor` through a **Load more files** control. It keeps only same-Workspace attachable text Resources, deduplicates by Resource ID while retaining the first revision shown to the user, preserves earlier results and the failed cursor for retry, and invalidates pending responses after picker close or Workspace/Conversation scope change. Repeated, empty, over-2,048-byte UTF-8 or non-advancing cursors stop with a recovery message instead of looping. The byte check now matches the Rust bridge's `String::len()` limit, including multi-byte cursors. Initial list failures can be retried. Exact refs are still reauthorized by the daemon at submit and dispatch.

TDD evidence: the page-merge/cursor helpers and the UTF-8 byte-limit regression were tested red before implementation, then `pnpm --dir apps/litecowork-ui exec node --experimental-transform-types --test tests/conversation-attachments.test.ts` passed **6/6**. The helper cases cover same-Workspace filtering, deduplication with first-seen revision preservation, cursor advancement, exhaustion, loop rejection and the multibyte byte-limit edge. `conversation-empty-state-truthfulness.test.mjs` passed **7/7** source guards. These do not mount React or exercise a network failure/retry through the rendered picker.

Updated the Feature 001 T14 task, the T21 attachment trace, WIRING, and the current implementation status to replace the stale first-page-only finding. T14 remains PARTIAL: first-message attachments, root ingestion, ContextAttachment/Context Used, binary input, cross-platform/provider E2E and qualification remain open. The existing Saved Views decision is a separate TARGET/IN_REVIEW design and remains gated for Feature 007 cross-owner review and Feature 016 T01/T02.

## 2026-11-17 continuation — Feature 001 T21 Runtime readiness trace

The source audit verified the Settings > Runtime status path end to end: `Refresh Status` invokes Tauri `get_runtime_status`, then the Tauri lifecycle bridge validates the current Runtime/incarnation identity, obtains the validated local Operator connection, and reads `GET /v1/runtime-lifecycle`. Settings renders the observed Operator/execution state, recovery state and safe active-blocker message/resolution hint.

Cross-checking that path against the Conversation composer exposed a real fail-closed gap. Settings derives `executionReady` from Operator readiness plus `recoveryState === READY`; `ConversationsPage` receives only `operatorReady`, and `isSendEligible` does not consume lifecycle state. When the Operator remains serving while recovery is `DEGRADED`, Settings can show execution closed while Send remains eligible for an otherwise eligible binding. The Conversation UI contract and WIRING now record this source-backed gap against AC-016/T09; no unauthorized UI behavior was changed.

The same reconciliation records the T08 Workspace catalog enumeration regression: both startup recovery sweeps fail on `list_workspaces()`, startup records `EXPIRED_EXECUTION_LEASE_RECOVERY_FAILED` and `CONVERSATION_SESSION_RECOVERY_FAILED`, persists `DEGRADED`, and exposes lifecycle blockers before Operator admission. The two regressions passed in the prior T08 verification. T21 remains PARTIAL because first-send, ambiguous replay, lifecycle gating, provider behavior and packaged restart/qualification traces remain incomplete. This slice changed the Feature 001 UI behavior contract and WIRING only, plus the T21/T22 status records. No provider, UI or integration test was run for this documentation-only reconciliation. Structural checks passed: `python .specify/scripts/validate_feature_package.py` (20/20 packages; structural only), `python scripts/audit_served_routes.py` (183 OpenAPI / 4 outside / 51 target-only), `python scripts/audit_v1_markdown.py` (372 files / 79,109 lines / 8,214,711 bytes / 0 broken links), WIRING YAML parse, and `git -c core.whitespace=cr-at-eol diff --check` (exit 0; existing LF-to-CRLF warnings only). Nothing was staged, committed or pushed.

## Prior code checkpoint — 2026-11-17 (T20 verification; next P1 path blocked on provider auth)

The source-backed T20 re-audit found that durable dispatch-intent ownership was claimed only after host and binding/profile preflight. A second Runtime could therefore fail preflight and settle a turn already claimed by the first Runtime. The Operator now passes its local Runtime incarnation through `AgentTurnCoordinator`; the supervisor claims before preflight, and only a confirmed claimant may settle. A confirmed loser is a no-op; an ambiguous claim or pre-claim worker panic leaves the intent for startup recovery. The SQLite-backed losing-dispatch regression was observed failing before the fix (`FAILED` instead of `OPEN`) and now passes. Native-Windows `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked conversation_dispatch_ -- --nocapture` passed **11/11**; `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked conversation_turn -- --nocapture` passed **19/19**; the route handoff test passed **1/1**; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed. The task's `cargo clippy -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked -- -D warnings` gate **failed before reaching the daemon crate** on seven pre-existing Clippy diagnostics in `crates/windows-local-security/src/lib.rs` (not in T20's allowed paths). T20 remains PARTIAL: a claim-error attempt remains OPEN until the startup sweep; live in-process reconciliation, packaged multi-process replay and provider qualification remain open. This is CODE only.

The re-audit found that the first T20 routing condition was unchecked despite current source routing submit/retry through `AgentTurnCoordinator::dispatch_admitted_turn`; only `turn_coordinator.rs` calls `AgentSessionSupervisor::dispatch_turn`. **T20 admission/outbox cross-check (2026-11-16; CODE):** the submit handler awaits the SQLite `create_conversation_turn` transaction before calling `AgentTurnCoordinator::dispatch_admitted_turn`; the transaction writes the USER message, OPEN turn, request receipt and UNCLAIMED dispatch intent atomically. On native Windows, `cargo test -j 1 -p storage-sqlite --locked conversation_turn_creation_rejects_a_second_nonterminal_turn_and_admits_after_settlement -- --nocapture` passed **1/1** (590 filtered), confirming a rejected concurrent admission leaves no partial records and a later settled Conversation can admit. `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked conversation_session_recovery_ -- --nocapture` passed **4/4** (525 filtered), including startup handling of a seeded prior-incarnation UNCLAIMED intent. This closes the bounded CODE check for commit-before-dispatch and unclaimed-intent startup recovery. It does not simulate a hard process kill, prove every crash window, or qualify a packaged/provider restart; those remain open under T08/T09.

**T20 exact-replay dispatch race correction (2026-11-16; CODE):** source re-audit found that submit idempotency replay calls the coordinator again. The durable intent CAS prevents two workers from both claiming, but it occurs after host/profile reads; a concurrent duplicate could therefore fail a lookup and settle the turn owned by the first worker. `AgentSessionSupervisor` now holds a Runtime-local owner keyed by Workspace, Conversation, turn and retry ordinal before spawning blocking work. A duplicate exact attempt returns without running pre-claim setup; the owner is moved into the blocking worker so HTTP waiter cancellation cannot release it while native work continues. The existing durable intent CAS remains the cross-process authority, and startup recovery still owns prior-incarnation intents. Native-Windows `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked conversation_dispatch_owner_registry_suppresses_exact_replay_until_worker_exits -- --nocapture` passed **1/1**; `conversation_dispatch_` passed **10/10** (520 filtered); `conversation_turn_submit_returns_accepted_receipt_with_active_session_supervisor` passed **1/1** (529 filtered); `conversation_session_recovery_` passed **4/4** (526 filtered). `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check apps/litecoworkd/src/agents/supervisor.rs` passed. The daemon test target rebuilt on native Windows with rust-lld in 32m35s. Existing unrelated daemon warnings remain. This is CODE evidence for the guard and adjacent failure/recovery paths, not an end-to-end duplicate-submit/provider journey or INTEGRATED/QUALIFIED evidence; T20 remains PARTIAL and its broad failure-settlement acceptance stays open.

## Latest T27 reconnect correction (2026-11-16; CODE)

The re-audit found an implementation gap against the existing T27 reconnect contract: the inline Conversation took an exact-attempt permission snapshot on active-turn entry and after sequence gaps, but not after a stream reconnect. A missed final permission or session-end frame could therefore leave the cached prompt actionable. `ConversationStreamController` now signals a resumed `stream.ready`; the Conversation page marks its exact attempt cache stale, which disables decisions and invokes the existing exact-attempt snapshot refresh. A failed refresh keeps the prompt disabled and exposes Check live status. This is implementation of the existing owner contract, not a new permission authority or schema.

## Feature 001 T08 final-response recovery regression (2026-11-16; CODE)

T08 is PARTIAL; the authorized path `apps/litecoworkd/src/runtime_recovery_tests.rs` now verifies the crash-after-final-commit recovery invariant. The versioned process identity and prior-incarnation host recovery helper have since been added and are wired before Operator admission; see the 2026-11-20 audit follow-up at the top of this file. The helper is CODE-level only and has not qualified process termination or packaged recovery.

The source audit followed Runtime startup through `recover_and_persist_startup_blockers` (`apps/litecoworkd/src/runtime.rs`), Conversation recovery (`apps/litecoworkd/src/agents/conversation_recovery.rs`), failure settlement (`apps/litecoworkd/src/agents/supervisor.rs`), and the SQLite session/host-binding transaction (`crates/storage-sqlite/src/conversations.rs`). At that checkpoint, startup reconciled expired execution leases and Conversation turns only. When a prior AgentSession was marked LOST, SQLite removed its AgentSessionHostBinding but left the old AgentHostInstance row/process reference without process verification or an explicit host transition.

At that historical checkpoint, `OpenCodeServer::process_identity_ref` (`apps/litecoworkd/src/agents/opencode_server.rs`) returned `pid:<pid>`, below the global Runtime lifecycle/data-model process-start identity and executable/owner evidence requirements. The child wrapper (`apps/litecoworkd/src/agents/mod.rs`) places Unix children into a process group and kills that group from `Drop`; a hard daemon kill skips that cleanup, so an orphan is possible by source-level inference, not demonstrated by a native crash test. Windows used `windows-local-security::spawn_kill_on_drop_job`, with Job Object kill-on-close behavior; at that time, the durable host row still needed startup reconciliation. This cross-platform difference is recorded in A-104. No stale-PID or process-name kill is authorized by the finding.

The stale Feature 001 PLAN risk row was aligned to the accepted recovery target: prior-incarnation Conversation work settles FAILED/LOST with `RUNTIME_RESTARTED`, and owned host processes require verified identity/provider reconciliation before replacement dispatch. T03/T08 remain PARTIAL. That source finding led to the user-delegated A-104 implementation, now CODE-wired before Operator admission. Native Windows/Linux hard-termination, PID-reuse, stale-row, retry and packaged recovery evidence remain qualification work; the earlier documentation-only T22 update did not itself implement the behavior.

The authenticated Operator POST validates the selected Workspace/Conversation, persisted RUNNING turn, ACTIVE AgentSession and Runtime identity, then binds the opaque prompt token and principal-scoped idempotency key to an in-memory bounded registry. It enqueues a bounded per-attempt command. The active OpenCode turn worker remains the sole owner of `OpenCodeServer`; its callback services commands before the next bounded provider poll. `ALLOW_ONCE` maps to the pinned `once` decision. `REJECT_SESSION_PENDING` maps to `reject` and updates known pending prompts in the same session. Any reply error causes one exact-session pending-list read; the code never repeats the reply POST. Confirmed still-pending requests clear the attempted decision and remain actionable; absent or unreadable state becomes `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`. Prompt storage is capped at 32 per attempt and 256 across retained Runtime scopes. Exact mailbox teardown removes that attempt's registry entries/tokens, preserving capacity for later attempts without evicting active scopes.

## Feature 001 T20 unavailable-host settlement retry (CODE; native Windows; 2026-11-15)

The source trace found that the no-eligible-host branch directly attempted `AGENT_UNAVAILABLE` settlement once, while other post-admission setup failures use the bounded transient-store retry helper. A transient local write error could therefore leave the admitted turn OPEN until startup recovery even though this outcome is known and no provider prompt was sent. Added a small closed-result helper that retries only the existing transient error set (up to three attempts, 100 ms backoff); persistent/permanent settlement failure returns `SETTLEMENT`, leaving the durable UNCLAIMED intent for recovery. The branch still returns success only after the typed `AGENT_UNAVAILABLE` settlement commits.

## Feature 001 T03 observed-exit retry after failed STOPPING write (CODE; native Windows; 2026-11-15)

The existing lost-READY-ack path could re-read a durable READY row and attempt READY→STOPPING before process cleanup, but if that first STOPPING write failed, an observed process exit fell through to the stale STARTING→FAILED compare-and-set. The host row could remain READY even though its process was confirmed exited. A related retry gap left a host DEGRADED after an earlier unconfirmed stop even when the caller's later stop observed exit, because retry settlement accepted STOPPING only. The host lifecycle helper now uses a bounded re-read/CAS reconciliation after observed exit: READY or DEGRADED advances through STOPPING to STOPPED; STARTING can settle FAILED; ambiguous acknowledgements count only after a re-read proves STOPPED/FAILED. The cleanup retry uses the same reconciliation. If storage remains unavailable or state cannot be confirmed within the bound, the outcome remains unconfirmed; no readiness is fabricated.

TDD evidence: `observed_exit_retries_ready_to_stopping_after_initial_write_failure` failed before the production change because the helper returned StateUnconfirmed and left the durable READY row. After the fix, native-Windows `$env:CARGO_TARGET_X86_64_PC_MSVC_LINKER = Join-Path (rustc --print sysroot) 'lib/rustlib/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc/bin/rust-lld.exe'; cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked agents::host_supervisor::tests -- --nocapture` passed **9/9**; the rebuilt test binary took 8m 45s and emitted 28 existing warnings. The T20 `conversation_dispatch_` filter passed **7/7**, and the adjacent `per_session_host_state_follows_observed_process_shutdown` test passed **1/1**; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed. Tests include the new READY-write failure and DEGRADED retry cases. These are helper CODE tests, not SQLite transition-fault injection, OpenCode process tests, restart recovery or provider qualification. No public API, event, schema, UI behavior or target state-machine decision changed; docs were aligned in the Feature 001 T03 TASKS/SPEC/RESEARCH/WIRING, implementation status and this handoff.

Documentation checks after the update: active corpus build reported 372 files / 77,646 lines; Markdown audit reported 369 files / 77,672 lines / 0 broken links; feature-package validation passed 20/20; design-owner consistency passed with 373 documents / 78,062 lines / 36 assertions / 0 issues; strict API audit reported 0 errors (231 OpenAPI operations: 178 served, 4 outside OpenAPI, 53 target-only); strict design-depth audit reported 0 findings (372 files / 77,646 lines / 12 domains / 254 functions / 201 settings / 231 routes / 107 tables / 20 packages). Architecture validation passed with one existing warning that optional `openapi-spec-validator` is not installed. CRLF-aware `git diff --check` passed; generated Markdown inventories were refreshed.

## Feature 001 T03 ambiguous READY cleanup reconciliation (CODE; native Windows; 2026-11-15)

Cross-layer tracing found the recording test store did not enforce the same expected-state compare-and-set as SQLite. After the fake was tightened, the new regression failed against the current production code: when READY had committed but returned an error, startup cleanup attempted `STARTING→FAILED`; SQLite correctly rejects that stale compare-and-set, leaving READY unchanged. The helper now re-reads the host. If READY committed, it attempts to write STOPPING before native cleanup; only a committed STOPPING row advances to STOPPED after an observed exit. If the first STOPPING write is not confirmed, an observed exit still triggers bounded state re-read/CAS recovery; after an unconfirmed stop, the caller retries once and reconciles from STOPPING or DEGRADED only when exit is observed. If storage cannot confirm, the row remains at its last confirmed state and the outcome is unconfirmed. A still-STARTING row follows its existing startup-failure/degraded path. If storage cannot confirm the lifecycle transition, the outcome remains unconfirmed and restart recovery is not established. No storage transition contract, API, event, schema, or UI was changed; the implementation follows the canonical `AgentHostInstance` state machine.

## Feature 001 T03 activation version propagation (CODE; native Windows; 2026-11-15)

Source trace confirmed the committed STARTING AgentSession store result was discarded and activation used a duplicated initial-version literal, with the started event revision separately hard-coded. The supervisor now passes the version returned by the committed reservation into the activation CAS and derives the ACTIVE revision using checked arithmetic. Zero, overflow, and a result outside SQLite's signed revision range fail closed before activation. The regression covers a non-initial starting version (17 → active/event revision 18), zero, and `u64` overflow.

## Feature 001 T20 blocking-worker recovery correction (CODE; native Windows; 2026-11-15)

Source tracing confirmed that early classified errors inside the `spawn_blocking` closure attempted to settle the exact attempt, but a `JoinError` escaped through `map_err(BlockingTask)` and the coordinator only wrote a diagnostic. A worker panic/cancellation could therefore leave an OPEN turn and CLAIMED intent until daemon restart. T20 permits exact-attempt settlement or a durable fence for explicit recovery. The supervisor now stores the Runtime-local AgentSession identity in an `Arc<OnceLock<_>>` outside the blocking worker before attempting STARTING reservation. If the worker fails, an independent blocking recovery uses that identity to settle the exact turn FAILED and close a committed session LOST; a failed recovery is reduced to `SETTLEMENT`, leaving the durable intent for existing startup recovery. The JoinError payload is never formatted into the structured diagnostic. Rust's process panic hook is unchanged, so the structured diagnostic redaction claim does not cover messages emitted by that hook.

The first SQLite-backed regression passed 1/1 for an admitted OPEN turn with no session. It was then strengthened to commit a real STARTING Conversation AgentSession before injecting the blocking worker panic. Final focused command: `$env:CARGO_TARGET_X86_64_PC_MSVC_LINKER = Join-Path (rustc --print sysroot) 'lib/rustlib/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc/bin/rust-lld.exe'; cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked conversation_blocking_dispatch_panic_settles_admitted_attempt` passed 1/1 (native Windows, real SQLite; turn FAILED, intent SETTLED, session LOST, USER retained, no AGENT message). At that earlier checkpoint, `conversation_dispatch_` passed 5/5 and `conversation_turn` passed 19/19; after the profile-lookup regression below, the dispatch filter passes 6/6. These checks do not qualify OpenCode or establish packaged UI/provider behavior. The `conversation_dispatch_` filter does not include the panic test because its name begins `conversation_blocking_`; the focused command above is its evidence.

This closes the specific same-runtime JoinError settlement gap in T20 CODE. Profile-catalog and binding-decode fault regressions now verify `ProfileLookup` and `BindingLookup` after successful host resolution; both settle the admitted turn FAILED and intent SETTLED without reserving an AgentSession. Native-Windows commands passed: focused `$env:CARGO_TARGET_X86_64_PC_MSVC_LINKER = Join-Path (rustc --print sysroot) 'lib/rustlib/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc/bin/rust-lld.exe'; cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked conversation_dispatch_binding_lookup_error_settles_admitted_attempt -- --nocapture` (1/1) and aggregate `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked conversation_dispatch_ -- --nocapture` (7/7). The focused binding test changes only the fixture's unused `configuration_json`, so host resolution still succeeds before full binding decoding fails. The earlier profile fixture attempt failed because SQLite foreign keys correctly prevented dropping the referenced profile table; FK enforcement is disabled only on that isolated test connection before dropping the catalog. T20 remains PARTIAL: exhaustive setup-stage coverage, service ownership extraction, shared-host/StopUnconfirmed lifecycle integration and packaged provider qualification remain open. The captured-writer regression verifies exact structured stderr bytes omit provider/secret detail; Rust's process panic hook and an external collector are not verified or changed. No runtime, API, event, schema or UI behavior changed in this slice; the added artifact is CODE regression coverage and its trace is reflected in T20/WIRING/status. ## Product direction clarified by the 2026-11-15 ChatGPT Work examples

The owner reiterated that LiteCowork should **supersede app switching for supported everyday work**, rather than become another specialist chat, coding, browser or file tool. The design intent is one conversation-led workspace with shared project/recents context and a contextual right panel that can show the live object needed for the current job (including an authorized live Computer preview), while keeping domain records and action authority with their owning services. This is a long-term product direction, not a claim that LiteCowork already replaces every desktop app or that V1 providers are qualified. The canonical product direction is now recorded in `docs/PRODUCT.md`; the contextual navigation, right-panel, preview and takeover details remain in the Feature 008/Workspace Experience proposal and require its recorded review gates. The screenshots repeat and clarify the existing design; they do not justify a second UI architecture or a product-mode switch. Documentation only; no UI/provider implementation or qualification was added in this note.

## Feature 001 T27 permission-prompt correction (CODE; native Windows; 2026-11-15)

Cross-checked Feature 001 T27 against the active SPEC/AC-031, the existing event/UI code, and the pinned OpenCode v2.0.32 permission routes and service. The accepted AC asks for inline + Needs You display and harness-mediated response; it does not require a timeout. Pinned OpenCode has session permission list/get/reply APIs; permission requests remain pending until a reply or service close, and service close rejects pending requests. `reject` rejects all currently pending requests for that native session, while `always` can save a persistent rule; neither scope is safe to hide behind a simple per-request label. No live same-session permission event/reply has been captured, so these are source-contract findings, not provider integration or qualification.

T27 acceptance/UI docs now state that LiteCowork must not invent an elapsed-time default, the request ID is Runtime-local and must not enter public activity, `always` is omitted from the basic prompt, and any reject UI must explain its session-wide effect. Source recheck found the native request ID was copied into activity `call_ref`; the recorder now emits permission activity with null `call_ref`, preserving reported activity without disclosing the reply key. The existing card represents a persisted REPORTED event, so the final copy labels it as a historical report and does not claim the native prompt is still open, timed out, or answered. No answer API, Needs You item, native reply, or restart/reconnect resolver was implemented.

TDD evidence: the original UI assertion failed on “the agent's own default applies.” The tightened historical-status assertion then failed on the old “remains pending” copy; after updating the copy and its test expectation, `node --experimental-transform-types --test tests/turn-activity.test.ts` passed 5/5. The daemon privacy assertion first failed because the frame exposed `perm-1`; after the recorder change, `$env:CARGO_TARGET_X86_64_PC_MSVC_LINKER = Join-Path (rustc --print sysroot) 'lib/rustlib/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc/bin/rust-lld.exe'; cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked turn_activity_records_publish_frames_and_persist_reported_rows_without_content -- --nocapture` passed 1/1 (native Windows). The daemon test-binary rebuild took 4m 34s and emitted existing unrelated warnings. Package-wide `pnpm test` completed with 614 passed, 7 failed, 5 skipped (626 total; 976.8 seconds; exit failure). The failures included Vite module-fetch and browser/CDP interaction timeouts plus the expected stale-copy assertion because the copy was edited during the full run; this run is not green. Focused CODE checks prove only this display/recording correction, not T27 completion or provider behavior.

## Earlier slice — Feature 001 T03 conservative host failure state (CODE; native Windows; 2026-11-15)

At the T03 checkpoint, the checkout was `main` at `ba50f41` (21 commits ahead of `origin/main`) with one worktree. Its then-current dirty-file count is historical and is superseded by the handoff inventory above. That slice directly changed the host helper and the owner/status/handoff documents listed below; the Markdown audit also refreshed its tracked report, inventory and cross-contract guard. No files were staged, committed, or pushed.

The READY commit failure path had a gap: if OpenCode process cleanup could not be observed, `start_host_process` returned the live process handle as `CleanupPending` but left the durable host row in its previous state. That could leave a live or uncertain process represented as STARTING, or as READY if the READY transaction committed but its acknowledgement was lost. The accepted AgentHostInstance state machine allows STARTING/READY/BUSY to transition to DEGRADED. The helper now re-reads the exact Runtime/incarnation/host row and best-effort transitions an extant live state to DEGRADED when cleanup is unconfirmed, preserving the private process identity reference. This adds no new state, public API, event, UI control or provider behavior; it is an internal T03 recovery correction.

RED/GREEN evidence: `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked unconfirmed_cleanup_after_ready_commit_failure_marks_host_degraded -- --nocapture` first ran but exposed a faulty fixture that did not inject READY-write failure; after adding the recording-store fault it failed as expected because no DEGRADED transition occurred. A test-only compile error from the recording-store shape was corrected before the valid RED run; no production change was involved in either fixture correction. After the helper change, the same focused test passed 1/1. The expanded PowerShell command `$env:CARGO_TARGET_X86_64_PC_MSVC_LINKER = Join-Path (rustc --print sysroot) 'lib/rustlib/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc/bin/rust-lld.exe'; cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked host_supervisor::tests -- --nocapture` passed 5/5, including the ambiguous lost-READY-ack case (`READY -> DEGRADED`). The build emitted existing unrelated daemon/test warnings. These are recording-store unit tests, not SQLite fault-injection, native process, OpenCode, or qualification evidence.

Updated owner/current evidence in Feature 001 TASKS, SPEC, RESEARCH, WIRING, `docs/CONTRADICTION-AND-ALIGNMENT-REGISTER.md`, `docs/SERVICES.md`, `docs/IMPLEMENTATION-STATUS.md` and this handoff. Remaining T03 gaps are unchanged: shared host ownership/reuse and retained leases, StopUnconfirmed process/session retention, idle TTL, daemon-restart process reconciliation, Runtime-neutral cwd, same-host multi-Workspace live proof, accepted Linux cgroup, and packaged native-provider qualification. The helper's best-effort transition can still fail if storage remains unavailable; startup reconciliation and retained process ownership are needed to close that case. Historical checkpoint before the 2026-11-19 A-101 correction: `ReadyHostStateGuard` shutdown ordering and SQLite direct-to-STOPPED edges were still open. T03 allowed paths have since been extended under USER-DELEGATED direction, the SQLite validator and orderly stop helper were corrected, and focused native-Windows regressions now pass. ## Feature 001 T03 per-session host lifecycle wired (CODE only; native Windows)

The authorized T03 helper is now wired into the OpenCode production dispatch path. Each per-session attempt gets a unique AgentHostInstance identity. `start_host_process` persists STARTING before native spawn, performs OpenCode startup and its pinned health check outside SQLite, persists the private Runtime-local process identity reference, then commits READY. Startup failure attempts STARTING→FAILED; if READY persistence fails, the child is stopped and the row becomes FAILED only when cleanup is confirmed, otherwise the helper re-reads state and best-effort marks it DEGRADED, including a lost READY acknowledgement. A source review found that successful response/cancellation storage settlement reset the host row to READY after the earlier process-stop code had marked it STOPPED. COMPLETED/CANCELLED/FAILED settlement now precedes per-turn process shutdown; the adapter reports whether exit was observed, then the supervisor records STOPPED or DEGRADED. An early-return guard attempts DEGRADED after the adapter drops its process.

This removes the old fabricated-READY insertion failure at source level, but remains a per-turn lifecycle, not the accepted shared AgentHostSupervisor. Source review found successful response/cancellation storage settlement reset the host row to READY after earlier code marked the process STOPPED. The flow now settles COMPLETED/CANCELLED/FAILED first, then stops the per-turn process and records STOPPED only on observed exit (DEGRADED otherwise). StopUnconfirmed still drops/stops the process and does not retain the process/session reference; there is no idle TTL, daemon-restart host reconciliation, neutral Runtime cwd or shared-host multi-root proof, and Unix still uses a process group rather than the accepted Linux cgroup. The absent-host dispatch fixture uses a Codex profile intentionally not Send-capable; it proves only that no fake READY host is inserted and failure settles after session reservation. It does not invoke OpenCode or qualify first-send. Previously recorded native Windows fixtures: host lifecycle helper 3/3; `conversation_dispatch_` 5/5; OpenCode adapter/server 52 passed with 1 manual profile smoke test ignored; selected adapter Conversation fixtures 13/13; explicit multi-Workspace location 1/1. New regression: `per_session_host_state_follows_observed_process_shutdown` passed 1/1. The initial `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked per_session_host_state_follows_observed_process_shutdown -- --nocapture` failed at the MSVC link step after `link.exe` exited unexpectedly at high memory use. Retrying with `$env:CARGO_TARGET_X86_64_PC_WINDOWS_MSVC_LINKER = (Join-Path (rustc --print sysroot) 'lib/rustlib/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc/bin/rust-lld.exe')` and the same cargo test passed 1/1 on native Windows; a preceding `cargo check -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked --tests` also passed. Existing unrelated warnings remain. These are CODE/fixture evidence only; no live OpenCode request/provider journey was run.

Post-change checks: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git -c core.whitespace=cr-at-eol diff --check` passed with only the existing line-ending normalization warnings; `python .specify/scripts/validate_feature_package.py` passed all 20 packages (structural validation only); `python scripts/validate_design_owner_consistency.py` read 373 documents / 77,918 lines and reported 36 assertions with zero issues; `python scripts/validate_architecture.py` passed its active checks with the existing optional `openapi-spec-validator` dependency warning; `python scripts/audit_v1_markdown.py` found zero broken links across 369 docs and generated the Markdown inventory at 77,528 lines / 7,729,530 bytes. These are code-format and contract/documentation checks, not integration or provider qualification.

## Feature 001 T03 pinned foreground-stop recheck (design evidence; no implementation change)

Rechecked official OpenCode v2.0.32 at tag commit `843a6c514e606b436a7fe713422ace0a93ad3d61`. LiteCowork starts the default foreground `serve` mode, whose main lifecycle waits indefinitely; the pinned protocol API groups do not expose a global-dispose route. The CLI's `service stop` invokes the managed service registry and is not a stop API for LiteCowork's private foreground child. The owner research and T03 task now record this exact distinction and the decision not to signal the user's separate service. A safe OS-owned graceful-stop operation and process-tree behavior remain unproven, so no host manager/TTL shutdown implementation is claimed. No OpenCode request was sent and no provider cost was incurred. Exact source links are in Feature 001 `RESEARCH.md` under “Foreground server shutdown boundary.”

Added `conversation_dispatch_host_instance_create_error_settles_unclaimed_attempt` in the authorized T20 adjacent-test scope. The regression asserts the current closed failure class (`HOST_INSTANCE_CREATE`), FAILED turn, SETTLED dispatch intent, and zero AgentSession rows. `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked conversation_dispatch_host_instance_create_error_settles_unclaimed_attempt -- --nocapture` passed 1/1 on native Windows. That finding has since been addressed by the T03 lifecycle helper above. A second issue in that same review—successful terminal settlement resetting the stopped host row to READY—was corrected in the current T03 slice by settling COMPLETED/CANCELLED/FAILED before per-turn shutdown. ## Feature 001 T04 session location request (CODE; Windows fake-transport evidence)

The T03/T04 cross-check showed that `OpenCodeServer::create_session` was deriving each native session's Workspace location from the server's process cwd. Added a failing transport regression first: two distinct temporary Workspace directories are sent through the same server handle and the captured session-create requests must preserve each exact `location.directory`. The initial test compile failed as expected because `create_session` had no per-session path parameter. `OpenCodeAdapter::create_session` now receives the revalidated Workspace path; the server rejects non-absolute, missing, non-directory or non-UTF-8 paths before dispatch and sends the explicit value in the OpenCode v2.0.32 session request.

Verification: `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked opencode_session_creation_sends_each_explicit_workspace_location -- --nocapture` passed 1/1 on native Windows; `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked selected_adapter_conversation -- --nocapture` passed 13/13; `cargo test -j 1 -p litecoworkd --locked opencode -- --nocapture` passed 52, with one manual profile smoke test intentionally ignored. `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `python .specify/scripts/validate_feature_package.py` (20 packages), `python scripts/validate_design_owner_consistency.py` (373 documents, 77,883 lines, 36 assertions, zero issues), `python scripts/validate_architecture.py`, and `git -c core.whitespace=cr-at-eol diff --check` passed. Architecture validation warns that `openapi-spec-validator` is not installed; its other checks completed. The first test build attempt hit Windows paging-file exhaustion while mapping an existing `storage_sqlite` artifact; the single-job rerun completed. Existing unrelated compiler warnings remain. This is CODE for LiteCowork request construction only: there is no live OpenCode server check of multi-root behavior, no shared host manager, and the process still starts per turn in the Workspace root. T03/T04 and provider qualification remain PARTIAL.

## T20 dispatch recovery regression added (CODE; 2026-11-14)

At that earlier T20 checkpoint, `conversation_dispatch_intent_claim_error_settles_unclaimed_attempt` was added in the authorized adjacent-test scope. A SQLite trigger injected a failure in the durable UNCLAIMED→CLAIMED write. The test confirmed the post-admission attempt settles FAILED, the dispatch intent settles, and no AgentSession is created for an unclaimed intent. The then-current aggregate filter passed 4/4 (host-resolution settlement, intent-claim settlement, claimed session-reservation settlement, and settlement-failure recovery after restart sweep). Subsequent checkpoints added blocking-worker, profile-catalog and binding-decode recovery and readable-but-missing pinned profile-row recovery; the current aggregate filter passes 9/9. These are CODE fixtures only; no first-send, provider integration, or qualification is established. Host-instance creation, exhaustive setup stages, external collector validation and packaged provider evidence remain open.

The T03 source review also exposed an implementation-scope dependency: safe host ownership must hold the process lease beyond per-turn adapter cleanup and through outcome settlement or Stop uncertainty. I cross-checked the accepted owner split in Feature 001 SPEC §7.2 and docs/SERVICES.md, then recorded the implementation seam in the owning SPEC, SERVICES, TASKS and WIRING: AgentHostSupervisor owns process lifetime and per-turn adapters borrow the transport; the already-authorized T04 adapter path is the paired integration path for T03. The internal adapter `shutdown` is turn-scoped cleanup; only AgentHostSupervisor stops the process. This does not change public ACP/OpenCode wire protocols. Feature 011 Runtime identity/recovery rows are present in source and Conversation host resolution already rejects non-READY or stale-incarnation Runtime offers; the concrete process lease/reconciliation behavior is still missing. Do not work around this by writing READY before observed startup, treating StopUnconfirmed as stopped, or moving process ownership into the session supervisor. Structural validation after this owner-doc update passed: feature-package validator (20 packages), design-owner consistency (373 docs / 77,872 lines / 36 assertions / 0 issues), architecture validator, Rust formatting and `git -c core.whitespace=cr-at-eol diff --check`.

**Follow-up T03/T04 source check (2026-11-14):** inspected the pinned official OpenCode v2.0.32 `Session.create` input and implementation. It requires an explicit `location` for a new session and resolves the project from `location.directory`. This led to the T04 request-construction change above: each adapter call now passes the revalidated root explicitly; the two-root fake transport regression passes. Feature 001 SPEC/TASKS/RESEARCH/WIRING retain the target requirement for a neutral Runtime-owned server cwd and real same-host two-root behavior tests (sequential and overlapping). Current provider process startup still uses the Workspace root and remains per turn. No shared host owner, leases, process-scope lifecycle or live provider test is implemented; no INTEGRATED or QUALIFIED claim is made.

## Latest user direction — universal workspace shell (product north star confirmed; exact V1 navigation remains TARGET / IN_REVIEW)

The owner reconfirmed that “supersede all” is the long-term product north star: remove app switching for supported everyday work by completing the workflow in LiteCowork's coherent conversation-led workspace, not specialize into separate chat/code/browser/file products or become a launcher directory. The supplied ChatGPT Work screenshots clarify the right-side pattern: contextual object tabs and actions to open/add work alongside the active conversation. The right side is a general object workspace populated by the current job (exact file/revision, diff, eligible public agent status, Browser/Computer/Terminal session, Canvas/Artifact or rendered answer) instead of a permanent row of specialist tools. The latest TARGET navigation revision prioritizes New Chat, Projects and unified Recents; Needs You is a compact count, infrequent domain views sit under More, and Settings remains at the bottom. This revises the earlier four-permanent-destination proposal, but the accepted V1 navigation baseline remains until the owning review is complete. **+ / New tab** is a searchable chooser; unavailable owner paths show their prerequisite. This is a product north star, not evidence that all app capabilities or providers are replaced or qualified in V1; native harness ownership and mediated-action boundaries remain in force. Product and V1 release contracts now distinguish the confirmed north star from unreviewed V1 navigation details.

This is a product/design TARGET update under Feature 008 revision 1.1 and T22, which remains PARTIAL pending owner review and unresolved decisions. It adds no API/schema/domain record and does not implement generic tabs or the revised navigation. Current `WorkbenchPanel` still has one local selected tab (`launcher`, `files`, `terminal`, `browser`, `diffs`, `activity`); no generic file editor, Subagents public-status view, live Computer preview or multi-object tab lifecycle is served. Tab selection/reopen must not grant permission, dispatch work, start capture/processes or restore authority. ACP has no universal Subagents feed; an external-app **Open with** handoff is deferred and outside the V1 promise. Native-agent identity, reasoning, sign-in, private transcripts and tools remain owned by their harness; direct unmediated actions remain outside LiteCowork Trust/Effect enforcement.

**Design work only:** updated Product, V1 Product, UI Screen, Workspace Experience Revision, Feature 008 README/SPEC/TASKS/UI behavior, Implementation Status, the dated re-audit addendum and this handoff with the compact navigation proposal and its relation to the accepted baseline. The design records the alternatives, selects one unified workspace target, and incorporates current official ChatGPT/Claude patterns without treating them as LiteCowork implementation evidence. The clarified “supersede all” direction is now stated as the long-term goal of reducing app switching through complete native workflows in one workspace; V1 and native-agent ownership boundaries remain explicit. Documentation validators most recently passed: Feature 001 and Feature 008 package validation (20 packages; structural only); design-owner consistency (373 docs, 77,808 lines, 36 assertions, zero issues); and `git -c core.whitespace=cr-at-eol diff --check`. Git emitted line-ending normalization warnings for two edited Markdown files and an already-dirty inventory CSV. These docs add no API/schema/domain record and change no CODE/SERVED/INTEGRATED/QUALIFIED evidence. A focused T20 daemon recovery test was attempted earlier, but its native link remained silent after compiler warnings; it was interrupted with no test result, and is not counted as passing or failing.

## Feature 001 T23/T27 pinned permission-contract recheck (source evidence only)

Re-read the official OpenCode `v2.0.32` permission schema, route and core service. Added the exact request payload and decision semantics to Feature 001 `RESEARCH.md`: `once` resolves one request, `reject` also rejects every currently pending request in that native session, and `always` may persist a rule and resolve other now-allowed requests. The pinned Permission service defines no timer/default for an unanswered prompt; service close rejects pending requests. This disproves T27's former “times out to harness default” acceptance wording. ACP permission requests remain a separate adapter path.

The finding is pinned source-contract evidence, not a live OpenCode capture, integrated LiteCowork route or qualification. `normalizer.rs` currently retains only the permission ID; current fixtures do not match the pinned payload; no Tauri/Operator answer route exists. T27 still needs a reviewed answer surface, same-session correlation, explicit session-wide rejection wording, no implicit persistent “always” action, response ambiguity handling and restart/session-close behavior. No source code changed in this slice; Feature 001 research is within T23's documented file scope. Feature 001 and 008 structural validators, design-owner consistency and the diff check passed after the edit.

## Active implementation slice — Feature 001 T04/T07 Stop-race outcome preservation

From the same `main` checkout at `ba50f41de170501e95c25d830e25f045fde9615f` (21 commits ahead of `origin/main`), I continued the authorized Feature 001 adapter slice. T04 is PARTIAL and explicitly allows `apps/litecoworkd/src/agents/adapter.rs` and adjacent adapter tests. I cross-checked the locked AC-010 rule, the provider normalizer contract, and the actual stream loop before editing.

- The cancellable OpenCode stream loop previously returned `Stopped` when it received `session.idle` after Stop, even if the same stream had delivered a completed text part; it also converted a correlated `session.error` during Stop into `Stopped`. Added two regression tests and confirmed RED: the adapter group had 11 passing tests and both new race cases failed with `Stopped`.
- The loop now preserves a completed non-empty `TextEnded` followed by `session.idle` as `Completed`, while a partial text delta plus idle remains `Stopped`; a correlated provider failure remains `Failed` even after Stop. The interrupt is still sent once, and missing quiescence still returns `StopUnconfirmed`.
- CODE evidence: `cargo test -p litecoworkd --locked selected_adapter_conversation_ -- --nocapture` passed 13/13; `cargo test -p litecoworkd --locked opencode -- --nocapture` passed 51, failed 0, ignored 1; `cargo test -p litecoworkd --locked conversation_stop_and_retry_failed_turn_lifecycle -- --nocapture` passed 1/1; `cargo test -p domain-conversation --locked` passed 8/8; full `cargo test -p litecoworkd --locked` passed 480 daemon tests, 0 failed, 1 ignored, plus 4 CLI tests. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git -c core.whitespace=cr-at-eol diff --check` passed. Builds emitted existing warnings in unrelated daemon modules.
- This is fixture-level CODE only. No live OpenCode event capture, packaged send/stop journey, SYSTEM run, or owner USER evidence was produced; T04 and T07 remain PARTIAL and no INTEGRATED/QUALIFIED claim is made.
- The T07 storage gap was confirmed RED by `completion_commit_after_stop_uses_the_cancel_request_version_and_event_edge`: a real SQLite Stop transition advanced activation version 2 to `CANCEL_REQUESTED` version 3, then the pinned completion commit returned `Conflict { expected: 2, actual: 3 }`. The storage transaction now accepts either the exact durable `CANCEL_REQUESTED` version or exactly one Stop increment from the activation version, uses the current durable version for its CAS and next version, and emits the truthful `CANCEL_REQUESTED -> COMPLETED` edge with the matching aggregate version. Any other stale/multi-step change still conflicts. This stayed within T07's explicit storage source/test paths.
- T07 CODE evidence: the new race test passed; `cargo test -p storage-sqlite --locked conversation_cancel_settles_cancelled_only_after_cancel_requested_and_keeps_racing_completion -- --nocapture` passed 1/1; full `cargo test -p storage-sqlite --locked` passed 588 unit tests, 2 registry-cache integration tests and 3 resilience tests, with 1 Windows Credential Manager test intentionally ignored. The full run emitted pre-existing unused-import/dead-code warnings in storage tests. Earlier T04 adapter evidence remains: the focused adapter group passed 13/13, OpenCode filter passed 51 with 1 ignored, and the full daemon package passed 480 daemon tests plus 4 CLI tests.
- T04/T07 remain PARTIAL and this is CODE evidence only. The adapter tests use synthetic fixtures; no pinned OpenCode event capture, packaged desktop send/stop/retry/restart journey, process-kill recovery, SYSTEM run, or owner USER evidence was produced. The accepted provider behavior and complete V1 Conversation qualification are not established by these tests. Do not claim INTEGRATED or QUALIFIED.
- Follow-up source check: quiescence timeout does not falsely settle CANCELLED; the adapter returns `StopUnconfirmed` and the supervisor leaves the turn `CANCEL_REQUESTED`. However, the supervisor's private `TurnOutcome::StopUnconfirmed` is swallowed by the outer dispatcher, and no durable activity/receipt or projected typed recovery field reaches the UI. The UI currently only sees `CANCEL_REQUESTED`. The existing activity schema has a closed kind/status enum with no recovery state, so exposing one requires an owner-approved contract/schema/UI trace change; T07's typed-recovery acceptance is not complete.

## Active implementation slice — Feature 001 T21 cross-layer trace re-audit

The current checkout remains on `main` at `ba50f41de170501e95c25d830e25f045fde9615f`, 21 commits ahead of `origin/main`, with the T14/T20 worktree changes preserved. - Added a separate New Chat first-Send gap trace: when no Conversation is selected, `sendTurn` creates an empty Conversation, returns without submitting a turn, and carries the text only in an in-memory map for a later second Send. The native Tauri create journal covers only the idempotent create request; no USER message, turn, dispatch intent or durable draft receipt is created by the first click.
- Added the existing-Conversation ambiguous-send gap: each invocation creates a fresh request ID; the UI appends a synthetic optimistic USER row; and the catch path restores the draft without reconciling the same request ID or explicitly removing the optimistic row. A transport error after durable admission is not distinguished from definite refusal, so a later fresh-key send can admit a duplicate after the first turn settles.
- Added the attachment-picker pagination gap: the daemon and Tauri bridge expose 100-item pages with `next_cursor`, while `openPicker` requests only the first page and discards the cursor. Resources beyond page one are not selectable from the Conversation composer. First-message attachments are also unavailable until a Conversation exists.
- Aligned WIRING: the UI-to-Operator create-only path is separate from turn admission; the post-commit handoff states that the supervisor claims the dispatch intent; the coordinator edge reflects typed JSON failure diagnostics; and the empty-Conversation create transaction is recorded separately from turn admission.
- These are source-backed gaps, not implementation changes. The T21 allowed paths do not authorize changing the UI or adding an implementation task. T09's current allowed scope only covers E2E files and the Send eligibility predicate; it cannot fix first-send behavior, ambiguous request recovery or attachment pagination. A separate owner-authorized task is needed for those fixes, so T21 remains PARTIAL. The UI action ledger's expected `AMBIGUOUS` state also needs reconciliation during T22 after T21 is complete.
- No product test was added or run for this documentation-only trace update. ## Active implementation slice — Feature 001 T20 dispatch failure settlement and diagnostics

The current checkout was verified on `main` at `ba50f41de170501e95c25d830e25f045fde9615f`, 21 commits ahead of `origin/main`, with one worktree. Feature 001 T20 is PARTIAL, depends on DONE T10, has no cross-package prerequisites, and explicitly permits the changed supervisor, coordinator, and adjacent test paths. Source review confirmed early post-admission setup errors could bypass settlement, and diagnostics derived a class from untrusted error text.

- `AgentSessionSupervisor::dispatch_turn` now returns only a closed `DispatchFailureClass`; host/binding/profile/host-instance/intent/session setup and provider failures no longer carry arbitrary error text into the coordinator. Provider details are discarded before reporting.
- Every setup/provider failure attempts to settle the exact turn and session. Transient storage settlement errors retry up to three times. A failed settlement emits only the `SETTLEMENT` class, leaving its durable intent for the existing startup recovery sweep. An absent eligible host is also settled and its settlement error is no longer discarded.
- Added a SQLite fault-injection test proving a host-resolution failure after admission makes the turn FAILED and the dispatch intent SETTLED. Added tests for closed structured diagnostics/no provider detail and transient/permanent settlement classification. Updated the existing retry lifecycle test to test a RUNNING conflict in a separate Conversation, removing a race with the newly settled retry.

## Latest continuation — design and source reconciliation — 2026-11-13

This documentation-only tranche resumes from HEAD `815ec0ffeb2e9b1f7fe55e602e049224f75826a7` and source checkpoint `6adadab539a62ca1c8b70621c7f88e1785cfe8c2`. It adds no product behavior. The generated TypeScript edit changes only an OpenAPI description string to match A-18.

The design records now make the product decisions explicit:

- **Workbench and Computer:** keep one compact left navigation and use the right Workbench for open objects. Show the selected laptop/window only after a local-view permit is already active and a Computer session is LIVE; that view selection starts no capture, grants no agent disclosure/input, steals no focus, changes no chat scroll and loses no composer draft. Respect a deliberate close for the session while leaving the control strip and **Show computer** entry available. This is TARGET/IN_REVIEW; Computer remains unavailable in the current UI.
- **Manual control:** use a persistent, non-modal **Take over / Stop run** strip while LiteCowork-mediated Computer input is agent-owned. Take over fences only Computer input and may leave agent reasoning active. Stop run additionally asks the owning Conversation or Task to cancel and reconcile. Neither action sends a chat message. A separate qualified shortcut/tray fence is required while the target app covers LiteCowork; without it, Computer input remains unavailable. Ordinary agent runs use their normal owner-routed Stop control.
- **Terminal:** create no process on app launch or when opening **+**. Start a terminal only through explicit **New terminal** in a qualified admitted Environment; keep user and Task sessions separate. The current terminal provider is unavailable, so default-profile behavior remains design, not a host-shell fallback.
- **Agent-generated UI:** keep one canonical validated PresentationIntent/RichPresentation path and host-owned registered components. An ACP agent may use a narrowly scoped proposal tool only after exact provider/version evidence; Rust binds and validates the result, and unsupported providers fall back to semantic text. Do not add arbitrary model HTML/JavaScript or a duplicate schema. Current A2UI research confirms protocol v1.0 is called stable, but official pages disagree about the exact React renderer release/support matrix; no package is pinned or adopted.
- **Feature 009:** revision 1.0 remains owner-accepted; revision 1.1 is IN_REVIEW. The manual-memory status/provenance conflict A-91 remains OPEN. The Coworker UI/API/wiring trace and transaction owners were reconciled, and all 11 package files are recorded at 1,401 lines with current hashes. Do not finalize v38 memory fields or Add Memory persistence before the owner decision.
- **Contract inventory:** current machine inventory is 231 OpenAPI operations: 178 mounted from OpenAPI, 4 served outside OpenAPI, 0 local-only, and 53 target-only. The three Feature 014 Conversation routes and the stream route need contract promotion; route registration is not qualification. Current Feature 001/002 task rows now use these counts; dated 2026-11-10 snapshots retain the values that were true at that time. The generated design-depth audit was refreshed to the current inventory.

The supplied competitor examples contributed interaction patterns, not an architectural replacement. OpenAI's current Data Analysis guidance shows interactive data tables with row/column/cell selection and CSV download, with only some chart types interactive; Claude's current Artifact guidance describes an editable surface beside conversation, templates and revisiting saved work. LiteCowork's accepted presentation slice remains narrower and has no general dataframe/query engine. Any broader file-analysis flow needs a Resource/dataset identity, exact selected-source receipts and an explicit user-approved context/send path.
